  *High-Yield Focus:* Innate non-human animal signaling (bee waggle dance, vervet monkey semantic predator calls) lacking true open-ended productivity, displacement, and infinite syntactic recursion characteristic of human universal grammar.
- [x] **855. Nonverbal Communication: Kinesics, Proxemics & Emotional Expression**   — `exam_ps90_7_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Cross-cultural universality of Ekman's six basic facial expressions (happiness, sadness, anger, fear, surprise, disgust); proxemic zones of interpersonal physical distance (intimate, personal, social, public); paralanguage vocal tone and prosody.

### Learning, Conditioning, Motivation & Emotion (Topics 856 - 895)

- [x] **856. Classical Conditioning Foundations: Pavlovian Paradigm Mechanics**   — `exam_ps90_8_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Unconditioned stimulus (UCS) evoking unconditioned response (UCR); pairing neutral stimulus (NS) with UCS transforms NS into conditioned stimulus (CS) eliciting conditioned response (CR); forward vs backward conditioning.
- [x] **857. Classical Conditioning Curves: Acquisition, Extinction & Spontaneous Recovery**   — `exam_ps90_8_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Gradual strengthening of CR during paired CS-UCS trials (acquisition); repeated CS presentation in absence of UCS leading to progressive reduction of CR (extinction); sudden reappearance of CR after a rest interval (spontaneous recovery).
- [x] **858. Stimulus Generalization vs Stimulus Discrimination in Conditioning**   — `exam_ps90_8_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Stimulus generalization: CR triggered by novel stimuli that share physical or perceptual similarity to the original CS along a stimulus gradient; Stimulus discrimination: learned capacity to differentiate CS from similar non-reinforced stimuli.
- [x] **859. Conditioned Taste Aversion & Biological Preparedness (Garcia Effect)**   — `exam_ps90_8_p2`
  *High-Yield Focus:* John Garcia's experiments demonstrating single-trial learned aversion to novel gustatory stimuli paired with delayed visceral nausea (hours later), violating standard temporal contiguity assumptions of behaviorism.
- [x] **860. Operant Conditioning: Reinforcement Contingencies (Positive vs Negative)**   — `exam_ps90_8_p3`
  *High-Yield Focus:* B.F. Skinner's operant conditioning; reinforcement increasing target behavior frequency: positive reinforcement (presentation of appetitive stimulus) vs negative reinforcement (removal/avoidance of aversive stimulus: escape vs avoidance learning).
- [x] **861. Operant Conditioning: Punishment Contingencies (Positive vs Negative)**   — `exam_ps90_8_p3`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Punishment decreasing target behavior frequency: positive punishment (application of aversive stimulus, e.g., shock, reprimand) vs negative punishment (withdrawal of rewarding stimulus, e.g., response cost, loss of privileges, time-out).
- [x] **862. Intermittent Reinforcement Schedules & Behavioral Extinction Rates**   — `exam_ps90_8_p4`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Fixed-Ratio (post-reinforcement pause), Variable-Ratio (highest rate of responding, highest resistance to extinction, gambling), Fixed-Interval (scalloped response pattern), and Variable-Interval (steady, moderate response rate).
- [x] **863. Operant Methodologies: Shaping Successive Approximations & Chaining**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Shaping: differentially reinforcing progressively closer behavioral approximations toward a complex terminal target behavior; Chaining: linking discrete established behaviors into an integrated sequential performance.
- [x] **864. Extinction Dynamics in Operant Conditioning: The Extinction Burst**   — `exam_ps90_8_p4`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Sudden temporary increase in response frequency, behavioral variability, and emotional aggression immediately following the abrupt cessation of expected reinforcement prior to eventual behavioral decline.
- [x] **865. Biological Constraints on Operant Behavior: Instinctive Drift**   — `exam_ps90_8_p5`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Keller and Marian Breland's observation of instinctive drift: operantly trained animals progressively abandoning learned instrumental responses to revert back to innate, evolutionary predisposed species-specific foraging behaviors.
- [x] **866. Observational Learning & Bandura's Social Cognitive Theory**   — `exam_ps90_8_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Albert Bandura's Bobo doll experiments demonstrating observational learning (modeling) without direct reinforcement; four core cognitive sub-processes: attention, retention, motor reproduction, and motivation/vicarious reinforcement.
- [x] **867. Neurobiology of Imitative Learning: The Mirror Neuron System**   — `exam_ps90_8_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Frontoparietal mirror neuron networks (ventral premotor cortex F5 and inferior parietal lobule) discharging both during the execution of goal-directed motor actions and during visual observation of another individual executing the same action.
- [x] **868. Learned Helplessness Paradigm & Depressive Attributional Styles**   — `exam_ps90_8_p7`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Martin Seligman's animal avoidance paradigms demonstrating exposure to uncontrollable, inescapable aversive electric shocks produces passive resignation; reformulation linking human depression to internal, stable, and global causal attributions.
- [x] **869. Drive-Reduction Theory of Motivation: Homeostatic Regulation**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Clark Hull's drive-reduction model: physiological deprivation produces biological needs that generate unpleasant states of psychological arousal (drives), motivating behavioral actions aimed at satisfying needs and restoring homeostatic equilibrium.
- [x] **870. Arousal Theory & The Yerkes-Dodson Law of Performance**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Inverted-U relationship between physiological/cortical arousal and behavioral performance efficiency; optimal performance achieved at moderate arousal levels; complex/unfamiliar cognitive tasks demanding lower arousal, simple tasks tolerating higher arousal.
- [x] **871. Maslow's Hierarchy of Needs & Humanistic Motivation**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Pyramid hierarchy: Physiological, Safety, Love/Belonging, Esteem, and Self-Actualization; prepotency principle requiring fulfillment of foundational deficiency needs before pursuing growth needs.
- [x] **872. Incentive Theory, Self-Determination Theory & Overjustification Effect**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Intrinsic vs extrinsic motivation; Self-Determination Theory (autonomy, competence, relatedness); overjustification effect where introducing extrinsic rewards diminishes baseline intrinsic motivation.
- [x] **873. Expectancy-Value Theory of Motivation**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Cognitive model positing behavioral motivation is a mathematical product of an individual's subjective expectancy of success and the intrinsic/extrinsic value assigned to achieving the goal.
- [x] **874. Opponent-Process Theory of Emotion & Acquired Motives**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Richard Solomon's model: primary emotional response (A-process) triggers an opposing, slower compensatory physiological response (B-process) that strengthens with repetition, explaining drug tolerance and addiction.
- [x] **875. Neuroendocrine Regulation of Hunger: Hypothalamic Feeding Centers**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Lateral hypothalamus (LH) hunger center (orexin/MCH, lesions cause aphagia) vs ventromedial hypothalamus (VMH) satiety center (lesions cause hyperphagia); arcuate nucleus neuropeptide circuitry.
- [x] **876. Gastrointestinal & Adipose Appetite Signals: Ghrelin, Leptin & PYY**   — `exam_ps90_9_disc / exam_ps60_9_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Gastric ghrelin stimulating orexigenic NPY/AgRP neurons; adipose leptin stimulating anorexigenic POMC/CART neurons signaling energy satiety; postprandial peptide YY (PYY) suppressing appetite.
- [x] **877. Physiological Regulation of Thirst: Osmotic vs Hypovolemic Mechanisms**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Osmoreceptors in the organum vasculosum of the lamina terminalis (OVLT) sensing plasma hypertonicity; arterial baroreceptors and juxtaglomerular renin release responding to hypovolemia via angiotensin II.
- [x] **878. Sexual Motivation: Masters & Johnson Human Sexual Response Cycle**   — `exam_ps90_8_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Four sequential physiological phases: Excitement, Plateau, Orgasm, and Resolution; male post-ejaculatory refractory period; hormonal influences of testosterone, estrogen, and oxytocin; medial preoptic area.
- [x] **879. Cognitive-Mediational Theory of Emotion: Lazarus Appraisal Paradigm**   — `exam_ps90_9_p1 / exam_ps60_9_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Richard Lazarus cognitive appraisal model: cognitive evaluation precedes affective and physiological arousal; primary appraisal (evaluating threat vs challenge) followed by secondary appraisal (assessing coping resources).
- [x] **880. Facial Feedback Hypothesis: Somatosensory Modulation of Affect**   — `exam_ps90_9_p1 / exam_ps60_9_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Subcortical and cortical feedback from voluntary or involuntary facial muscle contractions modulating subjective affective experience; Darwinian evolutionary roots of emotional facial signaling.
- [x] **881. Evolutionary Psychology of Emotion: Ekman's Universal Basic Emotions**   — `exam_ps90_9_disc / exam_ps60_9_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Paul Ekman's cross-cultural validation of six discrete basic emotions (happiness, sadness, anger, fear, disgust, surprise) characterized by distinct facial muscular configurations, autonomic signatures, and evolutionary survival functions.
- [x] **882. Neuroanatomy of Emotion: Limbic System & Subcortical Affective Circuits**   — `exam_ps90_9_p2 / exam_ps60_9_p2`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Hippocampus encoding episodic emotional context, anterior cingulate cortex processing emotional pain and conflict monitoring, insular cortex processing visceral somatic states and moral disgust.
- [x] **883. Cortical Regulation of Emotion: Ventromedial vs Dorsolateral Prefrontal Cortex**   — `exam_ps90_9_p2 / exam_ps60_9_p2`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Ventromedial prefrontal cortex (vmPFC) exerting top-down inhibitory control over amygdala reactivity and guiding emotional decision-making (Phineas Gage); dorsolateral PFC (dlPFC) executing cognitive reappraisal strategies.
- [x] **884. Stress Physiology: The Sympathomedullary (SAM) Neuroendocrine Axis**   — `exam_ps90_9_p3 / exam_ps60_9_p3`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Acute stressor triggering sympathetic splanchnic innervation of the adrenal medulla, rapid release of catecholamines (epinephrine, norepinephrine) mediating cardiovascular and metabolic fight-or-flight mobilization.
- [x] **885. Stress Physiology: Hypothalamic-Pituitary-Adrenal (HPA) Axis Dynamics**   — `exam_ps90_9_p3 / exam_ps60_9_p3`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Paraventricular CRH stimulating anterior pituitary ACTH release, triggering adrenal cortical cortisol secretion; systemic glucocorticoid metabolic mobilization, immunosuppression, and negative feedback loops.
- [x] **886. Hans Selye's General Adaptation Syndrome (GAS): Alarm, Resistance, Exhaustion**   — `exam_ps90_9_p4 / exam_ps60_9_p4`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Universal three-stage non-specific physiological response to sustained chronic stressors: initial Alarm reaction, compensated Resistance phase, and terminal Exhaustion phase leading to severe immunosuppression and allostatic collapse.
- [x] **887. Taxonomy of Stressors: Cataclysmic, Major Life Events & Daily Hassles**   — `exam_ps90_9_p5 / exam_ps60_9_p5`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Classification of environmental stressors: acute cataclysmic disasters, major personal life events (Holmes and Rahe SRRS life change units), chronic ambient stressors (pollution/noise), and daily micro-stressors (hassles).
- [x] **888. Coping Mechanisms: Problem-Focused vs Emotion-Focused Strategies**   — `exam_ps90_9_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Lazarus and Folkman transactional coping model: problem-focused coping aimed at altering the stressor or environment vs emotion-focused coping aimed at regulating internal emotional distress (reappraisal, distraction).
- [x] **889. Adaptive vs Maladaptive Coping Responses to Psychological Stress**   — `exam_ps90_9_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Adaptive coping: active cognitive reappraisal, seeking informational/emotional social support, mindfulness; Maladaptive coping: behavioral disengagement, denial, substance abuse, avoidance, and perseverative rumination.
- [x] **890. Allostatic Load & The Neurobiology of Psychological Resilience**   — `exam_ps90_9_p7`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Bruce McEwen's concept of allostatic load: the biological cost of chronic adaptation; hippocampal neurogenesis, prefrontal synaptic plasticity, and social connectedness acting as protective resilience factors.
- [x] **891. Frustration-Aggression Hypothesis & Berkowitz's Negative Affect Model**   — `exam_ps90_9_disc / exam_ps60_9_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Dollard's classical proposition that frustration inevitably generates aggressive drive; Leonard Berkowitz reformulation positing that any aversive stimulus generating negative affect primes hostile aggression via fight-or-flight networks.
- [x] **892. Psychological Defense Mechanisms: Psychoanalytic Ego Defenses**   — `exam_ps90_9_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Anna Freud's categorization of unconscious ego defenses: repression, suppression, denial, projection, rationalization, displacement, reaction formation, sublimation, intellectualization, and regression.
- [x] **893. Julian Rotter's Locus of Control: Internal vs External Expectancies**   — `exam_ps90_9_disc`  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Generalized expectancy regarding the causation of life outcomes: internal locus of control (believing outcomes result from personal skill and effort) vs external locus of control (attributing outcomes to chance, fate, or powerful others).
- [x] **894. Self-Efficacy Theory: Albert Bandura's Social Cognitive Construct**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc / exam_ps60_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Domain-specific belief in one's personal capability to execute courses of action required to attain designated performances; four sources: mastery experiences, vicarious modeling, verbal persuasion, and physiological arousal states.
- [x] **895. Self-Concept, Self-Schemas & Higgins' Self-Discrepancy Theory**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc / exam_ps60_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Multidimensional self-concept organized by cognitive self-schemas; E. Tory Higgins self-discrepancy framework: actual self vs ideal self (discrepancy generating depression) vs ought self (discrepancy generating anxiety).

### Developmental Psychology & Personality Theories (Topics 896 - 930)

- [x] **896. Piaget's Cognitive Developmental Stages: The Sensorimotor Period**   — `exam_ps90_10_p1 / exam_ps60_10_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Infant cognitive development (birth to 2 years): circular reactions, emergence of object permanence, stranger anxiety, and A-not-B search perseveration errors marking the dawn of internal mental representation.
- [x] **897. Piaget's Cognitive Developmental Stages: The Preoperational Period**   — `exam_ps90_10_p1 / exam_ps60_10_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Early childhood cognition (ages 2 to 7): rapid development of symbolic play and language, egocentrism (three-mountains task), centration, animistic thinking, and inability to perform mental operations or understand conservation.
- [x] **898. Piaget's Cognitive Developmental Stages: The Concrete Operational Period**   — `exam_ps90_10_p1 / exam_ps60_10_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Middle childhood cognition (ages 7 to 11): mastery of conservation (liquid volume, mass, number), reversibility of thought, decentration, operational classification hierarchies, and seriation of physical objects.
- [x] **899. Piaget's Cognitive Developmental Stages: The Formal Operational Period**   — `exam_ps90_10_p1 / exam_ps60_10_p1`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Adolescent cognition (ages 11 onward): hypothetico-deductive reasoning, abstract conceptualization, propositional logic, systematic isolation of experimental variables, and philosophical metacognition.
- [x] **900. Piagetian Constructivism: Schemas, Assimilation, Accommodation & Equilibration**   — `exam_ps90_10_p2 / exam_ps60_10_p2`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Cognitive adaptation via schemas: assimilation (incorporating new experiences into existing schemas) vs accommodation (modifying existing schemas to fit novel discrepant data); cognitive disequilibrium driving equilibration.
- [x] **901. Lev Vygotsky's Sociocultural Cognitive Development Theory**   — `exam_ps90_10_p3 / exam_ps60_10_p3`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Social origin of individual psychological processes: cultural tools and semiotic mediation, Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD), instructional scaffolding by more knowledgeable others, internalization of private speech.
- [x] **902. John Bowlby's Evolutionary Attachment Theory**   — `exam_ps90_10_p4 / exam_ps60_10_p4`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Innate evolutionary infant attachment behavioral system designed to maintain proximity to primary caregivers; concepts of the secure base, safe haven, separation protest, and internal working models of interpersonal relationships.
- [x] **903. Mary Ainsworth's Strange Situation Paradigm: Attachment Classifications**   — `exam_ps90_10_p4 / exam_ps60_10_p4`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Empirical assessment of 12-month infants: Secure attachment (distress at separation, comforted upon reunion), Insecure-Avoidant (minimal separation distress, reunion avoidance), Insecure-Resistant/Ambivalent (intense clingy distress, angry reunion), and Disorganized.
- [x] **904. Diana Baumrind's Typology of Parenting Styles**   — `exam_ps90_10_p5 / exam_ps60_10_p5`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Two-dimensional model of parental demandingness and responsiveness: Authoritative (high warmth, high control; optimal child outcomes), Authoritarian (low warmth, high control), Permissive (high warmth, low control), Neglectful/Uninvolved.
- [x] **905. Erikson's Psychosocial Stages: Infancy Through School Age**   — `exam_ps90_10_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Epigenetic developmental crises: Trust vs Mistrust (infancy, virtue: hope), Autonomy vs Shame/Doubt (toddler, virtue: will), Initiative vs Guilt (early childhood, virtue: purpose), Industry vs Inferiority (school age, virtue: competence).
- [x] **906. Erikson's Psychosocial Stages: Adolescence Through Old Age**   — `exam_ps90_10_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Epigenetic developmental crises: Identity vs Role Confusion (adolescence, virtue: fidelity), Intimacy vs Isolation (young adulthood, virtue: love), Generativity vs Stagnation (middle adulthood, virtue: care), Integrity vs Despair (late adulthood, virtue: wisdom).
- [x] **907. James Marcia's Four Identity Statuses in Adolescence**   — `exam_ps90_10_p6`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Cross-classification of identity exploration and commitment: Identity Diffusion (no exploration, no commitment), Identity Foreclosure (premature commitment without exploration), Identity Moratorium (active exploration, no commitment), Identity Achievement.
- [x] **908. Sigmund Freud's Structural Theory of Personality: Id, Ego, Superego**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc / exam_ps60_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Tripartite mental apparatus: Id (instinctual drives, pleasure principle, primary process), Ego (mediating reality principle, secondary process), Superego (internalized societal moral standards, conscience, and ego-ideal).
- [x] **909. Freud's Psychosexual Stages of Personality Development**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Oral (0-1 yr, oral fixations), Anal (1-3 yr, anal retentive vs expulsive personality), Phallic (3-6 yr, Oedipus/Electra complex, castration anxiety, penis envy), Latency (6-puberty), Genital (puberty onward, mature adult sexuality).
- [x] **910. Carl Jung's Analytical Psychology: Archetypes & The Collective Unconscious**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Personal unconscious vs collective unconscious containing inherited universal archetypes (Shadow, Anima/Animus, Persona, Self); psychological attitudes of extraversion vs introversion; individuation process.
- [x] **911. Alfred Adler's Individual Psychology: Inferiority Complex & Social Interest**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Psychological drive toward superiority and mastery compensating for universal childhood feelings of inferiority (inferiority complex); lifestyle development, teleological goals, and community feeling (Gemeinschaftsgefühl).
- [x] **912. Karen Horney's Feminist Critique & Neurotic Coping Trends**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Critique of Freudian male bias; basic anxiety resulting from parental indifference; three interpersonal neurotic trends: moving toward people (compliance), moving against people (aggression), moving away from people (detachment).
- [x] **913. Carl Rogers' Person-Centered Humanistic Personality Theory**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Organism's innate self-actualizing tendency; phenomenal field; conditions of worth imposed by parents; unconditional positive regard fostering congruence between the perceived real self and the idealized self.
- [x] **914. Gordon Allport's Idiographic Trait Hierarchy: Cardinal, Central, Secondary**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Pioneering trait theory: Cardinal traits (rare, all-encompassing dispositions dominating behavior), Central traits (5 to 10 prominent core personality building blocks), Secondary traits (situational, peripheral preferences).
- [x] **915. Raymond Cattell's Factor-Analytic Trait Architecture: The 16PF**   — `exam_ps90_10_disc`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Empirical psychometric factor analysis extracting 16 primary source traits (e.g., warmth, reasoning, emotional stability) underlying observable surface behaviors; objective assessment via 16 Personality Factor Questionnaire.
- [x] **916. Hans Eysenck's Biological PEN Model of Personality**   — `exam_ps90_10_p7`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Three orthogonal genetically grounded supertraits: Psychoticism (non-conformity, impulsivity), Extraversion (cortical arousal in ARAS), and Neuroticism (autonomic limbic emotional reactivity).
- [x] **917. The Five-Factor Model of Personality: The Big Five Traits (OCEAN)**   — `exam_ps90_10_p7`
  *High-Yield Focus:* Lexical hypothesis and cross-cultural factor analysis: Openness to experience, Conscientiousness, Extraversion, Agreeableness, and Neuroticism; empirical stability across adult lifespan and behavioral predictive validity.
- [ ] **918. Albert Bandura's Reciprocal Determinism in Personality**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Social cognitive perspective positing triadic reciprocal interaction: internal cognitive/biological factors, behavioral actions, and external environmental social cues continuously influencing each other dynamically.
- [ ] **919. Behavioral Genetics of Personality: Heritability Estimates & Twin Studies**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Monozygotic vs dizygotic twin paradigms and adoption studies estimating roughly 40-50% heritability across major personality dimensions; the dominant role of non-shared environmental influences over shared family environments.
- [ ] **920. Infant Temperament Classifications: Chess & Thomas to Kagan's Inhibition**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Chess and Thomas longitudinal categories (Easy, Difficult, Slow-to-warm-up); Jerome Kagan's behavioral inhibition paradigm demonstrating high motor and autonomic reactivity to novelty predicting later childhood social inhibition.
- [ ] **921. The Person-Situation Debate & Walter Mischel's Cognitive-Affective System**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Walter Mischel's critique of cross-situational trait consistency; Cognitive-Affective Personality System (CAPS) emphasizing situational behavioral signatures ('if situation X arises, then behavior Y follows').
- [ ] **922. Cognitive Changes in Aging: Fluid Decline vs Crystallized Maintenance**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Normal cognitive aging preserving crystallized verbal intelligence, procedural skills, and emotional regulation, alongside universal age-related slowing of mental processing speed, working memory, and episodic recall.
- [ ] **923. Infant Primitive Reflexes & Neurological Cortical Maturation**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Subcortical primitive reflexes (Moro startle, Palmar grasp, Rooting, Sucking, Babinski extensor plantar response); progressive disappearance and cortical inhibition during normal infant neurodevelopment.
- [ ] **924. Sequences of Motor Development: Cephalocaudal & Proximodistal Dynamics**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Universal developmental trajectories of motor milestones: cephalocaudal progression (head and neck motor control preceding trunk balance and bipedal locomotion) and proximodistal progression (core torso stability preceding fine pincer grasps).
- [ ] **925. Adolescent Neurobiology: Asynchronous Prefrontal & Limbic Maturation**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Neurodevelopmental imbalance: early maturation of hyper-responsive subcortical limbic reward networks outstripping protracted structural and functional myelination of prefrontal executive control circuits, driving sensation-seeking.
- [ ] **926. Gender Identity & Socialization: Kohlberg's Cognitive Developmental Theory**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Distinction between biological sex and gender identity; Kohlberg's developmental stages: gender identity/labeling (2-3 yrs), gender stability (3-4 yrs), and gender consistency/constancy (5-7 yrs); gender schema theory (Sandra Bem).
- [ ] **927. Agents of Socialization Across the Human Lifespan**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Lifelong process of internalizing cultural norms, values, and roles; primary socialization (family); secondary socialization (educational institutions, peer groups, mass media, workplace environments, religious institutions).
- [ ] **928. Total Institutions & The Dynamics of Resocialization (Erving Goffman)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Radical unlearning of old norms and adoption of new institutional identities within total institutions (prisons, military boot camps, psychiatric facilities); mortification of self rituals and degradation ceremonies.
- [ ] **929. Mechanisms of Cultural Transmission vs Cultural Diffusion**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Vertical cultural transmission (intergenerational enculturation of language, beliefs, and values within a specific society) vs horizontal cultural diffusion (spatial spread of cultural practices, ideas, or artifacts across distinct cultural groups).
- [ ] **930. Subcultures, Countercultures & Sociological Deviance**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Subcultures holding distinct cultural values within mainstream society; countercultures actively rejecting and subverting dominant societal hegemony; William Ogburn's cultural lag where non-material culture lags behind material technological advances.

### Psychological Disorders, Neuropathology & Treatment (Topics 931 - 960)

- [ ] **931. The Biopsychosocial Model of Psychological Disorders**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Integration of biological factors (genetics, neurotransmitters, neuroanatomy), psychological factors (cognitive appraisal, coping styles, learned behaviors), and socio-cultural factors (SES, systemic discrimination, culture-bound syndromes).
- [ ] **932. DSM-5 Diagnostic Framework: Categorical vs Dimensional Classification**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Diagnostic and Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders criteria; elimination of multiaxial system; tension between categorical diagnostic taxonomies (present/absent) and dimensional spectrum models of severity and symptom overlap.
- [ ] **933. Schizophrenia Spectrum Disorders: Positive Symptom Profiles**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Active psychotic manifestations: delusions of reference, persecution, or grandeur; hallucinations (auditory prominent); disorganized speech (derailment, tangentiality, word salad); grossly disorganized or bizarre catatonic motor behavior.
- [ ] **934. Schizophrenia Spectrum Disorders: Negative & Cognitive Symptom Profiles**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Deficit symptoms: affective flattening, avolition (apathy), alogia (poverty of speech), anhedonia, and social withdrawal; cognitive deficits in executive working memory, attentional gating, and processing speed.
- [ ] **935. Schizophrenia Etiology: Neurodevelopmental Hypothesis & Dopamine Circuitry**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Polygenic architecture, obstetric hypoxia, maternal viral infection; dopamine hypothesis: mesolimbic D2 hyperdopaminergia (positive symptoms) vs mesocortical D1 hypodopaminergia (negative symptoms); ventriculomegaly and cortical gray matter reduction.
- [ ] **936. Depressive Disorders: Major Depressive Disorder & Persistent Depressive Disorder**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Major Depressive Episode criteria (SIGECAPS: Sleep, Interest/anhedonia, Guilt, Energy, Concentration, Appetite/weight, Psychomotor agitation/retardation, Suicidal ideation); Persistent Depressive Disorder (dysthymia lasting >= 2 years).
- [ ] **937. Biochemical Bases of Mood Disorders: The Monoamine Hypothesis & Neuroplasticity**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Serotonin, norepinephrine, and dopamine neurotransmission deficits; delayed clinical efficacy of SSRIs/SNRIs pointing to downstream brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF) upregulation, hippocampal neurogenesis, and 5-HT receptor downregulation.
- [ ] **938. Bipolar & Related Disorders: Manic, Hypomanic & Depressive Episodes**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Bipolar I disorder (requiring at least one manic episode: DIG FAST criteria: Distractibility, Impulsivity, Grandiosity, Flight of ideas, Activity increase, Sleep deficit, Talkativeness); Bipolar II (hypomanic + major depressive episode); Cyclothymic disorder.
- [ ] **939. Anxiety Disorders: Generalized Anxiety Disorder & Panic Disorder**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Generalized Anxiety Disorder (pervasive, uncontrollable excessive worry lasting >= 6 months with somatic autonomic tension); Panic Disorder (recurrent unheralded panic attacks characterized by sympathetic surges and catastrophic fear of impending death).
- [ ] **940. Anxiety Disorders: Agoraphobia, Specific Phobias & Social Anxiety Disorder**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Agoraphobia (intense fear of environments where escape might be difficult); specific phobias (circumscribed irrational fears maintained via negative reinforcement avoidance); Social Anxiety Disorder (fear of negative evaluation in social performance situations).
- [ ] **941. Obsessive-Compulsive Disorder: Cortico-Striato-Thalamo-Cortical (CSTC) Loops**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Recurrent, intrusive distressing ego-dystonic thoughts, urges, or images (obsessions) generating severe distress neutralized by repetitive behavioral rituals or mental acts (compulsions); hyperactive CSTC orbitofrontal-caudate circuits.
- [ ] **942. OCD-Related Spectrum Disorders: Body Dysmorphic Disorder & Trichotillomania**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Body Dysmorphic Disorder (preoccupation with perceived defects in physical appearance unnoticeable to others, triggering repetitive mirror-checking); Trichotillomania (recurrent hair pulling); Excoriation (skin-picking disorder).
- [ ] **943. Trauma- and Stressor-Related Disorders: Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder (PTSD)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Development of four symptom clusters following exposure to actual/threatened death, serious injury, or sexual violence: intrusive re-experiencing (flashbacks, nightmares), persistent avoidance of trauma cues, negative alterations in cognitions/mood, and marked hyperarousal.
- [ ] **944. Acute Stress Disorder vs PTSD: Temporal Demarcation & Dissociation**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Diagnostic differentiation: Acute Stress Disorder symptoms manifesting between 3 days and 1 month post-trauma with prominent dissociative features; symptom persistence beyond 1 calendar month fulfilling clinical threshold for PTSD.
- [ ] **945. Dissociative Disorders: Dissociative Identity Disorder & Dissociative Amnesia**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Disruption of normal identity, memory, and consciousness integration; Dissociative Identity Disorder (presence of two or more distinct personality states linked to severe childhood trauma); Dissociative Amnesia (inability to recall personal autobiographical memories).
- [ ] **946. Depersonalization / Derealization Disorder**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Persistent or recurrent experiences of unreality or detachment from one's own mind, feelings, sensations, or bodily self (depersonalization) or from one's environmental surroundings (derealization), with intact reality testing.
- [ ] **947. Somatic Symptom Disorder & Illness Anxiety Disorder**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Somatic Symptom Disorder (prominent distressing physical somatic symptoms accompanied by excessive thoughts, anxiety, and devotion of time); Illness Anxiety Disorder (hypochondriasis: high health anxiety and preoccupation with acquiring severe illness in absence of somatic symptoms).
- [ ] **948. Conversion Disorder (Functional Neurological Symptom Disorder)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Sudden onset of altered voluntary motor or sensory function (e.g., psychogenic non-epileptic seizures, functional limb weakness, blindness) clinically incompatible with recognized neuroanatomical pathways; historical association with 'la belle indifférence'.
- [ ] **949. Feeding & Eating Disorders: Anorexia Nervosa vs Bulimia Nervosa**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Anorexia Nervosa (restriction of energy intake resulting in significantly low body weight, intense fear of gaining weight, disturbed self-body image); Bulimia Nervosa (recurrent binge eating episodes paired with inappropriate compensatory purging, laxatives, or fasting).
- [ ] **950. Binge-Eating Disorder & Avoidant/Restrictive Food Intake Disorder (ARFID)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Binge-Eating Disorder (recurrent episodes of consuming objectively large quantities of food accompanied by a sense of lack of control, without compensatory purging); ARFID (eating disturbance resulting in nutritional failure without body image distortion).
- [ ] **951. Personality Disorders Cluster A: Paranoid, Schizoid & Schizotypal**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Odd/eccentric cluster: Paranoid (pervasive unjustified mistrust and suspiciousness of others); Schizoid (pervasive pattern of social detachment and severely restricted emotional expression); Schizotypal (magical thinking, ideas of reference, and bizarre eccentric behavior).
- [ ] **952. Personality Disorders Cluster B: Antisocial, Borderline, Histrionic, Narcissistic**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Dramatic/emotional/erratic cluster: Antisocial (callous disregard for and violation of the rights of others, lack of remorse); Borderline (instability in interpersonal relationships, identity, and affect, frantic efforts to avoid abandonment, splitting); Histrionic; Narcissistic.
- [ ] **953. Personality Disorders Cluster C: Avoidant, Dependent & Obsessive-Compulsive**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Anxious/fearful cluster: Avoidant (pervasive social inhibition, feelings of inadequacy, extreme hypersensitivity to negative evaluation); Dependent (submissive clinging behavior, excessive need to be taken care of); Obsessive-Compulsive PD (pervasive perfectionism and rigid control).
- [ ] **954. Neurodevelopmental Disorders: Autism Spectrum Disorder (ASD)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Persistent social communication and interaction deficits across multiple contexts (social-emotional reciprocity, non-verbal cues) paired with restricted, repetitive patterns of behavior, interests, motor stereotypies, and hyper-/hypo-reactivity to sensory input.
- [ ] **955. Neurodevelopmental Disorders: Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (ADHD)**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Developmentally inappropriate patterns of inattention, hyperactivity, and impulsivity causing functional impairment; catecholaminergic (dopamine/NE) hypofunction in fronto-striatal circuits; therapeutic efficacy of stimulant pharmacotherapies.
- [ ] **956. Parkinson's Disease Neuropathology: Substantia Nigra & Alpha-Synuclein**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Progressive selective degeneration of dopaminergic neurons in the substantia nigra pars compacta projecting to the striatum; intracellular Lewy bodies composed of misfolded alpha-synuclein; motor hallmarks: resting tremor, cogwheel rigidity, bradykinesia, postural instability.
- [ ] **957. Huntington's Disease: CAG Trinucleotide Repeat Expansion & Chorea**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Autosomal dominant neurodegenerative genetic disorder caused by unstable CAG trinucleotide repeat expansion in the HTT gene; selective degeneration of GABAergic medium spiny projection neurons in the caudate and putamen; progressive motor chorea and cognitive dementia.
- [ ] **958. Cognitive-Behavioral Therapy (CBT) & Aaron Beck's Cognitive Triad**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Evidence-based psychotherapy identifying and restructuring distorted maladaptive automatic thoughts; Beck's negative cognitive triad (negative automatic views of the self, the world, and the future); behavioral activation and graded systematic exposure.
- [ ] **959. Psychodynamic vs Humanistic Psychotherapeutic Paradigms**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Psychodynamic therapy (bringing unconscious childhood conflicts, repressed defense mechanisms, and transference into conscious awareness) vs Client-Centered Humanistic therapy (Carl Rogers: non-directive active listening, unconditional positive regard, congruence, and empathy).
- [ ] **960. Biological Psychiatric Interventions: Psychopharmacology, ECT & TMS**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Pharmacotherapy: SSRIs, atypical second-generation antipsychotics, mood stabilizers (lithium carbonate); Electroconvulsive Therapy (ECT: medically induced generalized seizures for severe treatment-resistant depression); repetitive Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation (rTMS).

### Social Processes, Interpersonal Interaction, Culture & Stratification (Topics 961 - 1000)

- [ ] **961. Social Facilitation vs Social Inhibition: Zajonc's Drive Theory**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Presence of conspecifics elevating physiological arousal; enhancing performance of dominant, well-learned, or simple responses (social facilitation) while impairing performance of non-dominant, complex, or unpracticed tasks (social inhibition).
- [ ] **962. Deindividuation & Mob Psychology: The Stanford Prison Experiment**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Loss of individual self-awareness, moral restraint, and personal accountability within crowds; fueled by perceived anonymity, diffusion of responsibility, and group immersion; Zimbardo's Stanford Prison Experiment on institutional situational power.
- [ ] **963. Social Loafing Dynamics & The Ringelmann Effect**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Max Ringelmann's observation of diminished individual physical or cognitive effort when working collectively in a group toward a pooled shared goal compared to when working alone with individual accountability.
- [ ] **964. Group Polarization & Risky Shift Dynamics**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Deliberation within an ideologically homogeneous group shifting the collective post-discussion consensus toward a more extreme variant of the members' pre-existing inclinations; mediated by informational influence (arguments) and normative social influence (social comparison).
- [ ] **965. Irving Janis's Groupthink Model: Symptoms & Structural Flaws**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Maladaptive group decision-making process wherein excessive desire for group consensus overrides realistic critical appraisal of alternatives; symptoms: illusion of invulnerability, belief in inherent group morality, collective rationalization, out-group stereotyping, mindguards.
- [ ] **966. Sociology of Norms: Folkways, Mores, Taboos & Social Sanctions**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Spectrum of cultural norms: Folkways (informal conventions of daily etiquette; minor sanctions), Mores (morally significant norms essential to group welfare), Taboos (deeply institutionalized prohibitions evoking collective disgust); formal vs informal sanctions.
- [ ] **967. Edwin Sutherland's Differential Association Theory of Deviance**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Sociological proposition that deviant and criminal behavior is learned through symbolic interaction within intimate primary personal groups, wherein an individual internalizes an excess of definitions favorable to norm violation over unfavorable definitions.
- [ ] **968. Howard Becker's Labeling Theory: Primary vs Secondary Deviance**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Deviance not as an inherent property of an action, but as a consequence of societal social labeling; Edwin Lemert distinction: Primary deviance (initial norm violation that does not alter self-concept) vs Secondary deviance (adopting the deviant identity following societal stigmatization).
- [ ] **969. Robert Merton's Structural Strain Theory of Anomie**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Structural disconnect between culturally approved goals (e.g., American Dream wealth) and institutionalized legitimate means; five adaptive typologies: Conformity, Innovation (criminality), Ritualism, Retreatism (dropping out), and Rebellion.
- [ ] **970. Conformity Paradigms: Solomon Asch's Visual Line Judgment Studies**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Empirical demonstration of yielding to unanimous group consensus despite unambiguous sensory evidence; Normative social influence (conforming to gain social approval or avoid ostracism) vs Informational social influence (accepting group judgment as valid evidence of reality).
- [ ] **971. Obedience to Authority: Stanley Milgram's Electric Shock Paradigm**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Empirical study demonstrating ordinary individuals willing to administer dangerous, potentially lethal electrical shocks to an innocent confederate when instructed by an authoritative experimenter; variations showing effects of victim proximity, institutional prestige, and dissenting peers.
- [ ] **972. Social Compliance Strategies: Foot-in-the-Door vs Door-in-the-Face**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Sequential persuasion heuristics: Foot-in-the-Door (securing agreement to a small initial request significantly increases probability of later agreeing to a large target request) vs Door-in-the-Face (starting with an extreme request expected to be rejected, followed by target concession).
- [ ] **973. Social Compliance Strategies: Low-Ball & That's-Not-All Techniques**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Low-Ball technique (securing commitment to an attractive low-cost agreement, then revealing hidden costs or removing the initial incentive); That's-Not-All technique (offering an initial deal and sweetening the offer with added bonuses before the target can decide).
- [ ] **974. Harold Kelley's Covariation Model of Attribution**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Attributional framework assessing three dimensions: Consistency (does the person behave the same way over time?), Distinctiveness (does the person behave differently in other scenarios?), and Consensus (do other people behave the same way in this scenario?).
- [ ] **975. Attributional Biases: The Self-Serving Bias & Just-World Hypothesis**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Self-Serving bias (attributing personal successes to internal dispositional virtues and personal failures to external situational scapegoats); Melvin Lerner's Just-World Hypothesis (the cognitive belief that the world is inherently fair and people get what they deserve, driving victim-blaming).
- [ ] **976. Stereotyping, Prejudice & Discrimination: Cognitive-Affective-Behavioral Nexus**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* ABC model of intergroup bias: Stereotype (cognitive schema, overgeneralized belief about attributes of a group), Prejudice (affective negative evaluation or hostile emotional attitude toward a group), Discrimination (behavioral differential, unjustified treatment directed against group members).
- [ ] **977. Henri Tajfel's Social Identity Theory & In-Group Favoritism**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Self-concept derived from perceived membership in relevant social categories; social categorization, social identification, and social comparison; minimal group paradigm generating immediate in-group favoritism and out-group derogation; out-group homogeneity effect.
- [ ] **978. Muzafer Sherif's Realistic Conflict Theory & The Robbers Cave Experiment**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Intergroup prejudice and discrimination emerging directly from real or perceived zero-sum competition for scarce physical or economic resources; experimental resolution of intergroup hostility achieved solely through cooperative superordinate goals.
- [ ] **979. Social Cognitive Biases: The Halo Effect & Physical Attractiveness Stereotype**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Halo effect: cognitive tendency for an initial positive evaluation in one domain (e.g., physical beauty, charisma) to positively bias subsequent evaluations of unrelated traits (e.g., perceived moral character, intelligence, competence).
- [ ] **980. Impression Management Tactics & Self-Handicapping Strategies**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Deliberate strategic behaviors designed to regulate and control how one is perceived by others; tactical ingratiation, exemplification, and self-handicapping (preemptively manufacturing external impediments to success to preserve self-esteem and deflect failure attributions).
- [ ] **981. Interpersonal Attraction: Propinquity, Mere Exposure & Similarity**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Determinants of social attraction: physical proximity (propinquity effect), Robert Zajonc's mere exposure effect (repeated perceptual exposure to novel neutral stimuli increasing positive affective evaluation), and the powerful matching principle of cognitive/demographic similarity.
- [ ] **982. Evolutionary Altruism: Kin Selection, Reciprocal Altruism & Empathy**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* W.D. Hamilton's rule of inclusive fitness and kin selection (rB > C); Robert Trivers' reciprocal altruism (costly helping behaviors maintained through expectation of future reciprocal repayment); C. Daniel Batson's empathy-altruism hypothesis.
- [ ] **983. Determinants of Human Aggression: Biological, Psychological & Situational**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Biological bases: amygdala hyperactivity, prefrontal hypoactivity, elevated circulating testosterone, low serotonergic tone; Albert Bandura's observational social learning; situational triggers: physical pain, high ambient temperatures, overcrowding, and social rejection.
- [ ] **984. Sociological Structure: Ascribed, Achieved & Master Statuses**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Sociological statuses: Ascribed status (involuntary social position assigned at birth or later in life, e.g., sex, race, caste), Achieved status (voluntary social position attained via individual effort, talent, or choice), Master status (predominant status that completely overshadows all other identities).
- [ ] **985. Sociological Dynamics: Role Conflict, Role Strain & Role Exit**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Role set expectations; Role conflict (clash between the incompatible institutional demands of two or more distinct statuses held by one person, e.g., physician vs mother); Role strain (competing contradictory demands within a single status); Helen Ebaugh's stages of role exit.
- [ ] **986. Social Groups: Primary Groups, Secondary Groups & Reference Groups**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Charles Horton Cooley's distinction: Primary groups (small, intimate, enduring, emotionally significant face-to-face associations, e.g., family, close friends) vs Secondary groups (large, formal, impersonal, task-oriented, temporary); Reference groups used as comparative psychological benchmarks.
- [ ] **987. Max Weber's Bureaucracy: Characteristics of Rational-Legal Authority**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Ideal-type bureaucratic administration: jurisdictional competency, clear hierarchical chain of command, written documentation and explicit formal rules, division of labor, impersonal universal treatment, and merit-based qualification employment.
- [ ] **988. Dysfunctions of Bureaucracy: Parkinson's Law & The Iron Law of Oligarchy**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Parkinson's law (work expands so as to fill the time available for its completion); Robert Michels' Iron Law of Oligarchy (inevitable organizational drift toward concentrated oligarchical control by a self-perpetuating ruling elite, regardless of democratic origin).
- [ ] **989. George Ritzer's McDonaldization of Society**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Rationalization process wherein the organizational principles of fast-food restaurants come to dominate modern social institutions: Efficiency (optimal method), Calculability (quantification of value), Predictability (standardization), and Control via non-human technology; irrationality of rationality.
- [ ] **990. Sociological Theories of Aging: Disengagement, Activity & Continuity**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Theories of senescence: Disengagement theory (inevitable, mutual, functional withdrawal of aging individuals from societal roles); Activity theory (maintaining social activities, replacement roles, and community engagement optimizes healthy aging); Continuity theory.
- [ ] **991. Demographic Transition Theory: Four Structural Stages of Population Change**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Macro-sociological model of demographic evolution: Stage 1 (pre-industrial: high birth and high fluctuating death rates), Stage 2 (transitional: death rate plunges due to sanitation/medicine, birth rate remains high, population explosion), Stage 3 (industrial: birth rate drops), Stage 4 (post-industrial: low birth/death rates).
- [ ] **992. Thomas Malthus's Population Principle: Geometric vs Arithmetic Growth**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Malthusian theory: human population expands geometrically/exponentially (1, 2, 4, 8, 16) while food production expands only arithmetically (1, 2, 3, 4, 5); Malthusian catastrophe averted only by positive checks (famine, war, pestilence) or preventive checks (celibacy, late marriage).
- [ ] **993. Spatial Sociology: Urbanization, Suburbanization, Sprawl & Gentrification**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Population relocation to urban cores (urbanization); outward migration to metropolitan peripheries (suburban sprawl); gentrification (redevelopment of degraded urban neighborhoods attracting affluent capital, increasing property values, and displacing lower-income residents).
- [ ] **994. Systems of Stratification: Caste, Estate & Social Class Systems**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Structural inequality typologies: Caste system (hereditary, closed stratification with ascribed status, endogamy, and zero social mobility) vs Class system (open stratification based on economic resources, educational attainment, achieved status, and permeability for social mobility).
- [ ] **995. Socioeconomic Status (SES): Operationalization of Stratification**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Multidimensional sociological metric encompassing economic resources (current income and accumulated net wealth), educational attainment, and occupational prestige; socioeconomic gradient predicting health morbidity, life expectancy, and social capital access.
- [ ] **996. Forms of Social Mobility: Intergenerational, Intragenerational & Structural**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Directional mobility (upward vs downward); Intergenerational mobility (socioeconomic movement between parents and offspring) vs Intragenerational mobility (socioeconomic trajectory across an individual's career); Structural mobility driven by macroeconomic shifts.
- [ ] **997. Theories of Poverty: Absolute Poverty vs Relative Poverty & Marginalization**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Absolute poverty (objective biological deprivation: financial inability to secure basic life-sustaining resources like food, shelter, clean water) vs Relative poverty (subjective economic deprivation: living substantially below the median standard of living in one's society).
- [ ] **998. Social Exclusion, Residential Segregation & Spatial Inequality**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Social exclusion (systemic marginalization of vulnerable populations from full civic and economic participation); spatial inequality (geographic concentration of poverty, environmental toxins, and underfunded schools vs wealthy gated enclaves).
- [ ] **999. The Social Determinants of Health & Michael Marmot's Social Gradient**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Distal social determinants (housing, education, food security, chronic psychosocial stress); Sir Michael Marmot's Whitehall studies demonstrating an unbroken, step-wise socioeconomic gradient in cardiovascular mortality across all employment ranks.
- [ ] **1000. Kimberlé Crenshaw's Intersectionality Framework in Sociological Stratification**  
  *High-Yield Focus:* Analytical framework conceptualizing how multiple interlocking social identities and systems of oppression (race, gender, socioeconomic class, sexual orientation, disability) interact simultaneously to create unique, non-additive structures of systemic privilege and marginalization.
